{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-4-5-20250929","id":"msg_vrtx_01LWJ9evMmHr45kwy9hoaHQk","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01GHRNVjrcRACaDjJXJxs1Se","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/dc-opf-formulation.md","content":"# DC Optimal Power Flow (DC-OPF) Formulation\n\n## Overview\nDC Optimal Power Flow is a linearized approximation of the AC power flow equations used for economic dispatch and congestion management in power systems. It minimizes the total generation cost while respecting transmission constraints.\n\n## Key Assumptions\n1. Voltage magnitudes are close to 1.0 per unit\n2. Line resistances are small compared to reactances (R << X)\n3. Angle differences across lines are small\n4. Reactive power is ignored\n\n## Mathematical Formulation\n\n### Decision Variables\n- `Pg[i]`: Real power generation at generator i (MW)\n- `θ[n]`: Voltage angle at bus n (radians)\n\n### Objective Function\nMinimize total generation cost:\n```\nmin Σ C(Pg[i])\n```\n\nwhere C(Pg) is typically a quadratic cost function:\n```\nC(Pg) = c2 * Pg^2 + c1 * Pg + c0\n```\n\n### Constraints\n\n#### 1. Power Balance (Kirchhoff's Current Law)\nFor each bus n:\n```\nΣ Pg[i] - Pd[n] = Σ Pf[l]\n   i∈n              l from n\n```\n\nwhere:\n- Pg[i]: generation at bus n\n- Pd[n]: demand at bus n\n- Pf[l]: flow on lines connected to bus n\n\n#### 2. DC Power Flow Equation\nFor each branch from bus m to bus n:\n```\nPf[m→n] = B[m,n] * (θ[m] - θ[n])\n```\n\nwhere:\n- B[m,n] = 1/X[m,n] is the line susceptance\n- X[m,n] is the line reactance\n\n#### 3. Thermal Line Limits\nFor each branch:\n```\n-Pmax[l] ≤ Pf[l] ≤ Pmax[l]\n```\n\n#### 4. Generator Limits\nFor each generator:\n```\nPmin[i] ≤ Pg[i] ≤ Pmax[i]\n```\n\n#### 5. Reference Angle\nOne bus (slack bus) must have a fixed angle:\n```\nθ[slack] = 0\n```\n\n## Implementation with CVXPY\n\n```python\nimport cvxpy as cp\nimport numpy as np\n\ndef solve_dcopf(buses, generators, branches, gencosts, baseMVA=100):\n    # Number of entities\n    n_bus = len(buses)\n    n_gen = len(generators)\n    n_branch = len(branches)\n\n    # Decision variables\n    Pg = cp.Variable(n_gen)  # Generator outputs\n    theta = cp.Variable(n_bus)  # Bus voltage angles\n\n    # Build cost function (quadratic)\n    cost = 0\n    for i in range(n_gen):\n        # gencost format: [model, startup, shutdown, ncost, c2, c1, c0]\n        if gencosts[i][0] == 2:  # Polynomial cost\n            c2, c1, c0 = gencosts[i][4], gencosts[i][5], gencosts[i][6]\n            cost += c2 * cp.power(Pg[i], 2) + c1 * Pg[i] + c0\n\n    # Build incidence matrix and susceptance matrix\n    B_branch = np.zeros(n_branch)\n    from_bus = np.zeros(n_branch, dtype=int)\n    to_bus = np.zeros(n_branch, dtype=int)\n\n    for i, branch in enumerate(branches):\n        from_bus[i] = int(branch[0]) - 1  # Convert to 0-indexed\n        to_bus[i] = int(branch[1]) - 1\n        X = branch[3]  # Reactance\n        if X != 0:\n            B_branch[i] = 1.0 / X\n\n    # Constraints\n    constraints = []\n\n    # Generator limits\n    for i in range(n_gen):\n        constraints += [\n            Pg[i] >= generators[i][9],  # Pmin\n            Pg[i] <= generators[i][8]   # Pmax\n        ]\n\n    # Power balance at each bus\n    for n in range(n_bus):\n        # Sum of generation at bus n\n        gen_sum = 0\n        for i in range(n_gen):\n            if int(generators[i][0]) - 1 == n:\n                gen_sum += Pg[i]\n\n        # Demand at bus n\n        demand = buses[n][2]\n\n        # Net injection = generation - demand\n        net_injection = gen_sum - demand\n\n        # Flow out of bus\n        flow_out = 0\n        for l in range(n_branch):\n            if from_bus[l] == n:\n                flow_out += B_branch[l] * (theta[n] - theta[to_bus[l]])\n            elif to_bus[l] == n:\n                flow_out -= B_branch[l] * (theta[from_bus[l]] - theta[n])\n\n        constraints.append(net_injection == flow_out)\n\n    # Line thermal limits\n    for l in range(n_branch):\n        flow = B_branch[l] * (theta[from_bus[l]] - theta[to_bus[l]])\n        limit = branches[l][5]  # RATE_A\n        if limit > 0:\n            constraints += [flow <= limit, flow >= -limit]\n\n    # Reference angle (slack bus)\n    slack_bus = 0\n    for i, bus in enumerate(buses):\n        if bus[1] == 3:  # Slack bus type\n            slack_bus = i\n            break\n    constraints.append(theta[slack_bus] == 0)\n\n    # Solve\n    problem = cp.Problem(cp.Minimize(cost), constraints)\n    problem.solve(solver=cp.ECOS)\n\n    return {\n        'status': problem.status,\n        'cost': problem.value,\n        'Pg': Pg.value,\n        'theta': theta.value\n    }\n```\n\n## Locational Marginal Prices (LMPs)\n\nLMPs are the dual variables (shadow prices) of the power balance constraints. They represent the marginal cost of serving one additional MW of load at each bus.\n\n```python\n# Extract LMPs from dual variables\nlmps = [constraint.dual_value for constraint in power_balance_constraints]\n```\n\n## References\n- Wood, A. J., Wollenberg, B. F., & Sheblé, G. B. (2013). Power Generation, Operation, and Control\n- Zimmerman, R. D., Murillo-Sánchez, C. E., & Thomas, R. J. (2011). MATPOWER: Steady-State Operations, Planning, and Analysis Tools for Power Systems Research\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":5,"cache_creation_input_tokens":110,"cache_read_input_tokens":22790,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":110,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":18},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"2e57565f-babc-415b-b9d6-b0d1ae97df97","uuid":"739a500f-28f1-4125-a554-9a94b72fe515"}

{"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-4-5-20250929","id":"msg_vrtx_01Azz1MZNVf7xHsQ8Q2QLaEi","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_012YKDEsfxUZRuWjeqh6sqYt","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/reserve-cooptimization.md","content":"# Reserve Co-Optimization in Power Markets\n\n## Overview\nReserve co-optimization jointly optimizes energy and operating reserves to ensure system reliability while minimizing total cost. Spinning reserves provide the ability to respond to sudden generation or transmission outages.\n\n## Concept\nInstead of sequentially procuring energy then reserves, co-optimization considers both simultaneously, leading to more efficient market outcomes.\n\n## Mathematical Formulation\n\n### Additional Decision Variables\n- `Pg[i]`: Energy production from generator i (MW)\n- `R[i]`: Reserve provided by generator i (MW)\n\n### Modified Objective Function\n```\nmin Σ (C_energy(Pg[i]) + C_reserve(R[i]))\n```\n\nTypically, reserve costs are much lower than energy costs, often modeled as:\n```\nC_reserve(R) = λ_reserve * R\n```\n\nwhere λ_reserve is around 10-20% of the energy marginal cost.\n\n### Additional Constraints\n\n#### 1. Reserve Requirement\nTotal system reserves must meet minimum requirement:\n```\nΣ R[i] ≥ R_requirement\n```\n\n#### 2. Reserve Capacity Constraint\nEach generator has a maximum reserve capability:\n```\nR[i] ≤ R_max[i]\n```\n\n#### 3. Energy-Reserve Coupling (Headroom Constraint)\nA generator can only provide reserves if it has headroom:\n```\nPg[i] + R[i] ≤ Pmax[i]\n```\n\nThis is the \"Standard Capacity Coupling\" - the sum of energy production and reserve provision cannot exceed the generator's maximum capacity.\n\n#### 4. Minimum Generation for Spinning Reserve\nOnly online generators can provide spinning reserves:\n```\nR[i] ≤ M * status[i]\n```\n\nwhere status[i] ∈ {0,1} indicates if generator is online, and M is a large constant.\n\nFor generators already dispatched (Pg > Pmin), this is automatically satisfied.\n\n## Reserve Marginal Clearing Price (MCP)\n\nThe Reserve MCP is the dual variable (shadow price) of the system reserve requirement constraint. It represents the marginal cost of increasing the reserve requirement by 1 MW.\n\n## Implementation with CVXPY\n\n```python\nimport cvxpy as cp\n\ndef solve_dcopf_with_reserves(buses, generators, branches, gencosts,\n                                reserve_capacity, reserve_requirement,\n                                baseMVA=100):\n    n_gen = len(generators)\n\n    # Decision variables\n    Pg = cp.Variable(n_gen)\n    R = cp.Variable(n_gen)  # Reserve provision\n    theta = cp.Variable(len(buses))\n\n    # Objective: energy cost + reserve cost\n    energy_cost = 0\n    reserve_cost = 0\n\n    for i in range(n_gen):\n        # Energy cost (quadratic)\n        if gencosts[i][0] == 2:\n            c2, c1, c0 = gencosts[i][4], gencosts[i][5], gencosts[i][6]\n            energy_cost += c2 * cp.power(Pg[i], 2) + c1 * Pg[i] + c0\n\n        # Reserve cost (typically linear, ~10% of marginal energy cost)\n        # Use marginal cost at Pmin as approximation\n        reserve_price = gencosts[i][5] * 0.1  # 10% of linear coefficient\n        reserve_cost += reserve_price * R[i]\n\n    total_cost = energy_cost + reserve_cost\n\n    constraints = []\n\n    # Generator limits\n    for i in range(n_gen):\n        Pmin, Pmax = generators[i][9], generators[i][8]\n        constraints += [\n            Pg[i] >= Pmin,\n            Pg[i] <= Pmax\n        ]\n\n    # Reserve constraints\n    for i in range(n_gen):\n        # Non-negativity\n        constraints.append(R[i] >= 0)\n\n        # Reserve capacity limit\n        constraints.append(R[i] <= reserve_capacity[i])\n\n        # Energy-reserve coupling (headroom constraint)\n        constraints.append(Pg[i] + R[i] <= generators[i][8])\n\n    # System reserve requirement\n    reserve_req_constraint = cp.sum(R) >= reserve_requirement\n    constraints.append(reserve_req_constraint)\n\n    # ... (add DC-OPF constraints: power balance, line limits, etc.)\n\n    # Solve\n    problem = cp.Problem(cp.Minimize(total_cost), constraints)\n    problem.solve(solver=cp.ECOS)\n\n    # Extract reserve MCP\n    reserve_mcp = reserve_req_constraint.dual_value\n\n    return {\n        'status': problem.status,\n        'total_cost': problem.value,\n        'energy_cost': energy_cost.value,\n        'reserve_cost': reserve_cost.value,\n        'Pg': Pg.value,\n        'R': R.value,\n        'theta': theta.value,\n        'reserve_mcp': reserve_mcp\n    }\n```\n\n## Market Interpretation\n\n### Reserve MCP\n- **Positive MCP**: Reserve requirement is binding; increasing it would increase total cost\n- **Zero MCP**: Reserve requirement is not binding; system has excess reserve capability\n- **High MCP**: Indicates scarcity of reserve-capable resources\n\n### Generator Dispatch\nGenerators with low energy costs AND available headroom are preferred for energy. Generators with low opportunity costs (difference between max capacity and current dispatch) are preferred for reserves.\n\n## Benefits of Co-Optimization\n1. **Economic Efficiency**: Finds least-cost combination of energy and reserves\n2. **Better Resource Utilization**: Considers opportunity costs of reserve provision\n3. **Price Signals**: Provides clear price signals for both energy and reserves\n4. **Reliability**: Ensures adequate reserves while maintaining economic dispatch\n\n## References\n- Ortega-Vazquez, M. A., & Kirschen, D. S. (2009). \"Estimating the Spinning Reserve Requirements in Systems With Significant Wind Power Generation Penetration\"\n- FERC Order 755 (2011): Frequency Regulation Compensation\n- PJM Manual 11: Energy & Ancillary Services Market Operations\n"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":4,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1818,"cache_read_input_tokens":22900,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":1818,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":18},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"2e57565f-babc-415b-b9d6-b0d1ae97df97","uuid":"4f1bf2a5-a614-4c4c-9ba1-2e692b017e6f"}

Spinning reserves provide the ability to respond to sudden generation or transmission outages.\n\n## Concept\nInstead of sequentially procuring energy then reserves, co-optimization considers both simultaneously, leading to more efficient market outcomes.\n\n## Mathematical Formulation\n\n### Additional Decision Variables\n- `Pg[i]`: Energy production from generator i (MW)\n- `R[i]`: Reserve provided by generator i (MW)\n\n### Modified Objective Function\n```\nmin Σ (C_energy(Pg[i]) + C_reserve(R[i]))\n```\n\nTypically, reserve costs are much lower than energy costs, often modeled as:\n```\nC_reserve(R) = λ_reserve * R\n```\n\nwhere λ_reserve is around 10-20% of the energy marginal cost.\n\n### Additional Constraints\n\n#### 1. Reserve Requirement\nTotal system reserves must meet minimum requirement:\n```\nΣ R[i] ≥ R_requirement\n```\n\n#### 2. Reserve Capacity Constraint\nEach generator has a maximum reserve capability:\n```\nR[i] ≤ R_max[i]\n```\n\n#### 3. Energy-Reserve Coupling (Headroom Constraint)\nA generator can only provide reserves if it has headroom:\n```\nPg[i] + R[i] ≤ Pmax[i]\n```\n\nThis is the \"Standard Capacity Coupling\" - the sum of energy production and reserve provision cannot exceed the generator's maximum capacity.\n\n#### 4. Minimum Generation for Spinning Reserve\nOnly online generators can provide spinning reserves:\n```\nR[i] ≤ M * status[i]\n```\n\nwhere status[i] ∈ {0,1} indicates if generator is online, and M is a large constant.\n\nFor generators already dispatched (Pg > Pmin), this is automatically satisfied.\n\n## Reserve Marginal Clearing Price (MCP)\n\nThe Reserve MCP is the dual variable (shadow price) of the system reserve requirement constraint. It represents the marginal cost of increasing the reserve requirement by 1 MW.\n\n## Implementation with CVXPY\n\n```python\nimport cvxpy as cp\n\ndef solve_dcopf_with_reserves(buses, generators, branches, gencosts,\n                                reserve_capacity, reserve_requirement,\n                                baseMVA=100):\n    n_gen = len(generators)\n\n    # Decision variables\n    Pg = cp.Variable(n_gen)\n    R = cp.Variable(n_gen)  # Reserve provision\n    theta = cp.Variable(len(buses))\n\n    # Objective: energy cost + reserve cost\n    energy_cost = 0\n    reserve_cost = 0\n\n    for i in range(n_gen):\n        # Energy cost (quadratic)\n        if gencosts[i][0] == 2:\n            c2, c1, c0 = gencosts[i][4], gencosts[i][5], gencosts[i][6]\n            energy_cost += c2 * cp.power(Pg[i], 2) + c1 * Pg[i] + c0\n\n        # Reserve cost (typically linear, ~10% of marginal energy cost)\n        # Use marginal cost at Pmin as approximation\n        reserve_price = gencosts[i][5] * 0.1  # 10% of linear coefficient\n        reserve_cost += reserve_price * R[i]\n\n    total_cost = energy_cost + reserve_cost\n\n    constraints = []\n\n    # Generator limits\n    for i in range(n_gen):\n        Pmin, Pmax = generators[i][9], generators[i][8]\n        constraints += [\n            Pg[i] >= Pmin,\n            Pg[i] <= Pmax\n        ]\n\n    # Reserve constraints\n    for i in range(n_gen):\n        # Non-negativity\n        constraints.append(R[i] >= 0)\n\n        # Reserve capacity limit\n        constraints.append(R[i] <= reserve_capacity[i])\n\n        # Energy-reserve coupling (headroom constraint)\n        constraints.append(Pg[i] + R[i] <= generators[i][8])\n\n    # System reserve requirement\n    reserve_req_constraint = cp.sum(R) >= reserve_requirement\n    constraints.append(reserve_req_constraint)\n\n    # ... (add DC-OPF constraints: power balance, line limits, etc.)\n\n    # Solve\n    problem = cp.Problem(cp.Minimize(total_cost), constraints)\n    problem.solve(solver=cp.ECOS)\n\n    # Extract reserve MCP\n    reserve_mcp = reserve_req_constraint.dual_value\n\n    return {\n        'status': problem.status,\n        'total_cost': problem.value,\n        'energy_cost': energy_cost.value,\n        'reserve_cost': reserve_cost.value,\n        'Pg': Pg.value,\n        'R': R.value,\n        'theta': theta.value,\n        'reserve_mcp': reserve_mcp\n    }\n```\n\n## Market Interpretation\n\n### Reserve MCP\n- **Positive MCP**: Reserve requirement is binding; increasing it would increase total cost\n- **Zero MCP**: Reserve requirement is not binding; system has excess reserve capability\n- **High MCP**: Indicates scarcity of reserve-capable resources\n\n### Generator Dispatch\nGenerators with low energy costs AND available headroom are preferred for energy. Generators with low opportunity costs (difference between max capacity and current dispatch) are preferred for reserves.\n\n## Benefits of Co-Optimization\n1. **Economic Efficiency**: Finds least-cost combination of energy and reserves\n2. **Better Resource Utilization**: Considers opportunity costs of reserve provision\n3. **Price Signals**: Provides clear price signals for both energy and reserves\n4. **Reliability**: Ensures adequate reserves while maintaining economic dispatch\n\n## References\n- Ortega-Vazquez, M. A., & Kirschen, D. S. (2009). \"Estimating the Spinning Reserve Requirements in Systems With Significant Wind Power Generation Penetration\"\n- FERC Order 755 (2011): Frequency Regulation Compensation\n- PJM Manual 11: Energy & Ancillary Services Market Operations\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

It allows you to express optimization problems in a natural way and automatically transforms them into a form that can be solved by various solvers.\n\n## Installation\n```bash\npip install cvxpy\n```\n\nFor specific solvers:\n```bash\npip install cvxpy[CBC]     # Open-source MILP solver\npip install cvxpy[GLPK]    # GNU Linear Programming Kit\npip install mosek          # Commercial solver (free academic license)\npip install gurobipy       # Commercial solver (free academic license)\n```\n\n## Basic Usage Pattern\n\n```python\nimport cvxpy as cp\nimport numpy as np\n\n# 1. Define decision variables\nx = cp.Variable()           # Scalar variable\ny = cp.Variable(10)         # Vector variable (10 elements)\nZ = cp.Variable((5, 5))     # Matrix variable (5x5)\n\n# 2. Define objective function\nobjective = cp.Minimize(cp.sum_squares(y) + x)\n\n# 3. Define constraints\nconstraints = [\n    x >= 0,\n    y >= -1,\n    y <= 1,\n    cp.sum(y) == 5\n]\n\n# 4. Create and solve problem\nproblem = cp.Problem(objective, constraints)\nproblem.solve()\n\n# 5. Access results\nprint(f\"Status: {problem.status}\")\nprint(f\"Optimal value: {problem.value}\")\nprint(f\"Optimal x: {x.value}\")\nprint(f\"Optimal y: {y.value}\")\n```\n\n## Common Operations\n\n### Variable Creation\n```python\n# Scalar\nx = cp.Variable()\nx = cp.Variable(nonneg=True)  # Non-negative\nx = cp.Variable(pos=True)      # Strictly positive\n\n# Vector\ny = cp.Variable(n)\ny = cp.Variable(n, nonneg=True)\n\n# Matrix\nZ = cp.Variable((m, n))\nS = cp.Variable((n, n), symmetric=True)  # Symmetric matrix\n```\n\n### Parameters (Constants)\n```python\n# Use parameters for constants that might change\na = cp.Parameter()\nb = cp.Parameter(n)\nA = cp.Parameter((m, n))\n\n# Set values\na.value = 3.5\nb.value = np.array([1, 2, 3, 4, 5])\nA.value = np.random.randn(m, n)\n```\n\n### Objective Functions\n```python\n# Minimization\nobjective = cp.Minimize(expression)\n\n# Maximization\nobjective = cp.Maximize(expression)\n\n# Common expressions\ncp.sum(x)                    # Sum of elements\ncp.sum_squares(x)            # Sum of squares\ncp.norm(x, 2)                # Euclidean norm\ncp.norm(x, 1)                # L1 norm\ncp.quad_form(x, P)           # x.T @ P @ x\n```\n\n### Constraints\n```python\n# Equality\nconstraints = [A @ x == b]\n\n# Inequality\nconstraints = [\n    x >= 0,           # Element-wise for vectors\n    x <= u,\n    A @ x <= b\n]\n\n# Combining constraints\nconstraints = [\n    x >= 0,\n    cp.sum(x) == 1,\n    A @ x <= b\n]\n```\n\n## Extracting Dual Variables (Shadow Prices)\n\nDual variables represent the marginal value of relaxing a constraint.\n\n```python\n# Define constraint and keep reference\nconstraint = cp.sum(x) >= 10\nconstraints = [constraint, x >= 0]\n\n# Solve problem\nproblem = cp.Problem(objective, constraints)\nproblem.solve()\n\n# Access dual variable\nshadow_price = constraint.dual_value\nprint(f\"Shadow price: {shadow_price}\")\n```\n\nFor multiple constraints:\n```python\n# List of constraints\npower_balance = []\nfor i in range(n_buses):\n    c = (generation[i] - demand[i] == net_injection[i])\n    power_balance.append(c)\n    constraints.append(c)\n\n# Solve\nproblem.solve()\n\n# Extract dual variables (LMPs in power systems)\nlmps = [c.dual_value for c in power_balance]\n```\n\n## Solver Selection\n\n```python\n# Automatically select solver\nproblem.solve()\n\n# Specify solver\nproblem.solve(solver=cp.ECOS)      # For convex problems\nproblem.solve(solver=cp.SCS)       # Scalable for large problems\nproblem.solve(solver=cp.OSQP)      # For QP problems\nproblem.solve(solver=cp.GLPK_MI)   # For MILP\nproblem.solve(solver=cp.GUROBI)    # Commercial solver\nproblem.solve(solver=cp.MOSEK)     # Commercial solver\n\n# With solver options\nproblem.solve(solver=cp.ECOS, max_iters=1000, abstol=1e-8)\n```\n\n## Checking Problem Status\n\n```python\nproblem.solve()\n\nif problem.status == cp.OPTIMAL:\n    print(\"Optimal solution found\")\n    print(f\"Optimal value: {problem.value}\")\nelif problem.status == cp.INFEASIBLE:\n    print(\"Problem is infeasible\")\nelif problem.status == cp.UNBOUNDED:\n    print(\"Problem is unbounded\")\nelse:\n    print(f\"Solver status: {problem.status}\")\n```\n\n## Common Patterns for Power Systems\n\n### Quadratic Cost Minimization\n```python\n# min c2*P^2 + c1*P + c0\nP = cp.Variable(n_gen)\ncost = cp.sum([c2[i] * cp.power(P[i], 2) + c1[i] * P[i] + c0[i]\n               for i in range(n_gen)])\n```\n\n### Power Balance Constraints\n```python\n# For each bus: generation - demand = net injection\nfor bus_id in range(n_buses):\n    gen_at_bus = cp.sum([P[i] for i in gens_at_bus[bus_id]])\n    constraints.append(gen_at_bus - demand[bus_id] == injection[bus_id])\n```\n\n### Line Flow Limits\n```python\n# |B * (theta_from - theta_to)| <= limit\nfor line in range(n_lines):\n    flow = B[line] * (theta[from_bus[line]] - theta[to_bus[line]])\n    constraints += [\n        flow <= limit[line],\n        flow >= -limit[line]\n    ]\n```\n\n## Performance Tips\n\n1. **Use vectorization**: Operate on vectors/matrices instead of loops when possible\n2. **Avoid nonlinear operations**: Stick to convex operations (linear, quadratic, norms)\n3. **Use appropriate solver**: ECOS for general convex, OSQP for QP, GLPK for LP\n4. **Warm start**: Reuse solutions when solving similar problems repeatedly\n5. **Parameter objects**: Use `cp.Parameter` for values that change between solves\n\n## Example: Complete Quadratic Program\n\n```python\nimport cvxpy as cp\nimport numpy as np\n\n# Problem data\nn = 10\nP = np.random.randn(n, n)\nP = P.T @ P  # Make positive semidefinite\nq = np.random.randn(n)\nG = np.random.randn(5, n)\nh = np.random.randn(5)\nA = np.random.randn(2, n)\nb = np.random.randn(2)\n\n# Decision variable\nx = cp.Variable(n)\n\n# Objective: minimize (1/2)x'Px + q'x\nobjective = cp.Minimize(0.5 * cp.quad_form(x, P) + q.T @ x)\n\n# Constraints: Gx <= h, Ax = b\nconstraints = [G @ x <= h, A @ x == b]\n\n# Solve\nproblem = cp.Problem(objective, constraints)\nproblem.solve(solver=cp.OSQP)\n\nprint(f\"Status: {problem.status}\")\nprint(f\"Optimal value: {problem.value}\")\nprint(f\"Optimal x: {x.value}\")\n```\n\n## References\n- Official documentation: https://www.cvxpy.org/\n- CVXPY tutorial: https://www.cvxpy.org/tutorial/\n- Diamond, S., & Boyd, S. Its data format is widely used for representing power system networks in research and education. The format uses matrices to represent buses, generators, branches, and costs.\n\n## File Structure\nA MATPOWER case file contains several key matrices stored as arrays:\n- `bus`: Bus data (loads, voltages)\n- `gen`: Generator data (capacity, output)\n- `branch`: Transmission line data (impedance, limits)\n- `gencost`: Generator cost curves\n- `baseMVA`: Base MVA for per-unit system (typically 100)\n\n## Bus Matrix Format\nEach row represents one bus with 13 columns:\n\n| Column | Name      | Description                           | Units  |\n|--------|-----------|---------------------------------------|--------|\n| 0      | BUS_I     | Bus number (positive integer)         | -      |\n| 1      | BUS_TYPE  | 1=PQ, 2=PV, 3=Slack/Reference         | -      |\n| 2      | PD        | Real power demand                     | MW     |\n| 3      | QD        | Reactive power demand                 | MVAr   |\n| 4      | GS        | Shunt conductance                     | MW@1pu |\n| 5      | BS        | Shunt susceptance                     | MVAr@1pu|\n| 6      | BUS_AREA  | Area number                           | -      |\n| 7      | VM        | Voltage magnitude                     | p.u.   |\n| 8      | VA        | Voltage angle                         | degrees|\n| 9      | BASE_KV   | Base voltage                          | kV     |\n| 10     | ZONE      | Loss zone                             | -      |\n| 11     | VMAX      | Maximum voltage magnitude             | p.u.   |\n| 12     | VMIN      | Minimum voltage magnitude             | p.u.   |\n\n### Example\n```python\nbus = [\n    # BUS_I, TYPE, PD,  QD,  GS, BS, AREA, VM,  VA, BASE_KV, ZONE, VMAX, VMIN\n    [1,      3,    0,   0,   0,  0,  1,    1.0, 0,  138,     1,    1.1,  0.9],\n    [2,      1,    150, 50,  0,  0,  1,    1.0, 0,  138,     1,    1.1,  0.9],\n]\n```\n\n## Generator Matrix Format\nEach row represents one generator with 10 columns:\n\n| Column | Name       | Description                      | Units |\n|--------|------------|----------------------------------|-------|\n| 0      | GEN_BUS    | Bus number                       | -     |\n| 1      | PG         | Real power output (setpoint)     | MW    |\n| 2      | QG         | Reactive power output            | MVAr  |\n| 3      | QMAX       | Maximum reactive power           | MVAr  |\n| 4      | QMIN       | Minimum reactive power           | MVAr  |\n| 5      | VG         | Voltage magnitude setpoint       | p.u.  |\n| 6      | MBASE      | Machine MVA base                 | MVA   |\n| 7      | GEN_STATUS | 1=in service, 0=out of service   | -     |\n| 8      | PMAX       | Maximum real power output        | MW    |\n| 9      | PMIN       | Minimum real power output        | MW    |\n\n### Example\n```python\ngen = [\n    # BUS, PG,  QG, QMAX, QMIN, VG,  MBASE, STATUS, PMAX, PMIN\n    [1,    100, 0,  200,  -100, 1.0, 100,   1,      250,  10],\n    [2,    50,  0,  100,  -50,  1.0, 100,   1,      150,  0],\n]\n```\n\n## Branch Matrix Format\nEach row represents one transmission line or transformer with 13 columns:\n\n| Column | Name      | Description                          | Units   |\n|--------|-----------|--------------------------------------|---------|\n| 0      | F_BUS     | \"From\" bus number                    | -       |\n| 1      | T_BUS     | \"To\" bus number                      | -       |\n| 2      | BR_R      | Resistance                           | p.u.    |\n| 3      | BR_X      | Reactance                            | p.u.    |\n| 4      | BR_B      | Total line charging susceptance      | p.u.    |\n| 5      | RATE_A    | Long term rating (thermal limit)     | MVA     |\n| 6      | RATE_B    | Short term rating                    | MVA     |\n| 7      | RATE_C    | Emergency rating                     | MVA     |\n| 8      | TAP       | Transformer tap ratio (0=no xfmr)    | -       |\n| 9      | SHIFT     | Transformer phase shift              | degrees |\n| 10     | BR_STATUS | 1=in service, 0=out of service       | -       |\n| 11     | ANGMIN    | Minimum angle difference             | degrees |\n| 12     | ANGMAX    | Maximum angle difference             | degrees |\n\n### Example\n```python\nbranch = [\n    # F, T,  R,      X,     B,   RATE_A, RATE_B, RATE_C, TAP, SHIFT, STATUS, ANGMIN, ANGMAX\n    [1,  2,  0.001,  0.01,  0.0, 100,    120,    150,    0,   0,     1,      -30,    30],\n    [2,  3,  0.002,  0.02,  0.0, 80,     100,    120,    0,   0,     1,      -30,    30],\n]\n```\n\n### Important Notes\n- `RATE_A` (column 5) is the primary thermal limit used in DC-OPF\n- For DC-OPF, only reactance (`BR_X`) is used; resistance is ignored\n- If `RATE_A = 0`, the line is considered to have no thermal limit\n\n## Generator Cost Matrix Format\nEach row represents the cost curve for one generator:\n\n| Column | Name     | Description                              |\n|--------|----------|------------------------------------------|\n| 0      | MODEL    | 1=piecewise linear, 2=polynomial         |\n| 1      | STARTUP  | Startup cost                             |\n| 2      | SHUTDOWN | Shutdown cost                            |\n| 3      | NCOST    | Number of cost coefficients              |\n| 4+     | COST     | Cost coefficients (highest order first)  |\n\n### Polynomial Cost Model (MODEL = 2)\nFor a quadratic cost function: `Cost = c2*P^2 + c1*P + c0`\n\n```python\ngencost = [\n    # MODEL, STARTUP, SHUTDOWN, NCOST, c2,    c1,   c0\n    [2,      0,       0,         3,     0.01,  20.0, 100.0],\n]\n```\n\nThis represents: `Cost(P) = 0.01*P^2 + 20*P + 100` ($/hr)\n\n### Piecewise Linear Cost Model (MODEL = 1)\nDefined by points `(P1, C1), (P2, C2), ..., (Pn, Cn)`\n\n```python\ngencost = [\n    # MODEL, STARTUP, SHUTDOWN, NCOST, P1, C1,   P2,  C2,   P3,  C3\n    [1,      0,       0,         3,     0,  100,  50,  600,  100, 1200],\n]\n```\n\n## Loading MATPOWER Data in Python\n\n```python\nimport json\nimport numpy as np\n\ndef load_matpower_json(filename):\n    \"\"\"Load MATPOWER case from JSON file\"\"\"\n    with open(filename, 'r') as f:\n        data = json.load(f)\n\n    return {\n        'baseMVA': data['baseMVA'],\n        'bus': np.array(data['bus']),\n        'gen': np.array(data['gen']),\n        'branch': np.array(data['branch']),\n        'gencost': np.array(data['gencost']),\n        # Optional fields\n        'reserve_capacity': data.get('reserve_capacity', []),\n        'reserve_requirement': data.get('reserve_requirement', 0)\n    }\n\n# Usage\ncase = load_matpower_json('network.json')\nn_buses = len(case['bus'])\nn_gens = len(case['gen'])\ntotal_demand = sum(bus[2] for bus in case['bus'])  # Sum of PD\n```\n\n## Accessing Specific Elements\n\n```python\n# Find all generators at a specific bus\nbus_id = 5\ngens_at_bus = [i for i, gen in enumerate(case['gen']) if gen[0] == bus_id]\n\n# Find a specific branch\nfrom_bus = 10\nto_bus = 20\nbranch_idx = None\nfor i, branch in enumerate(case['branch']):\n    if (branch[0] == from_bus and branch[1] == to_bus) or \\\n       (branch[0] == to_bus and branch[1] == from_bus):\n        branch_idx = i\n        break\n\n# Get thermal limit of a branch\nif branch_idx is not None:\n    thermal_limit = case['branch'][branch_idx][5]  # RATE_A\n\n# Calculate total generation capacity\ntotal_capacity = sum(gen[8] for gen in case['gen'])  # Sum of PMAX\n```\n\n## Per-Unit System\nMATPOWER uses per-unit representation where:\n- Power base = `baseMVA` (typically 100 MVA)\n- Actual MW = per-unit value × baseMVA\n- Impedance: `Z_pu = Z_actual × baseMVA / (kV_base^2)`\n\nFor most problems with `baseMVA = 100`, power values are already in MW (not per-unit), so no conversion is needed.\n\n## Common Operations\n\n```python\n# Total system demand\ntotal_demand = sum(bus[2] for bus in case['bus'])\n\n# Average generator cost (linear term)\navg_cost = np.mean([gc[5] for gc in case['gencost'] if gc[0] == 2])\n\n# Number of binding lines (at thermal limit)\nbinding_count = sum(1 for branch in case['branch'] if branch[5] > 0)\n\n# Slack bus identification\nslack_bus_idx = next(i for i, bus in enumerate(case['bus']) if bus[1] == 3)\n```\n\n## References\n- MATPOWER Manual: https://matpower.org/docs/MATPOWER-manual.pdf\n- Zimmerman, R. D., Murillo-Sánchez, C. E., & Thomas, R. J. {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-sonnet-4-5-20250929","id":"msg_vrtx_01QydfAyy2PWm9YJeJMDFgX5","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_vrtx_01RCdZ9khH6S7iLDVRZ1ps3F","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/root/environment/skills/lmp-calculation.md","content":"# Locational Marginal Pricing (LMP) in Power Systems\n\n## Overview\nLocational Marginal Prices (LMPs) represent the cost to serve one additional MW of load at each bus in the power system. They are fundamental to electricity market operations and congestion management.\n\n## Definition\nThe LMP at bus n is:\n```\nLMP[n] = ∂(Total Cost) / ∂(Load[n])\n```\n\nIn optimization terms, LMPs are the dual variables (shadow prices) of the power balance constraints.\n\n## Components of LMP\nLMP can be decomposed into three components:\n\n```\nLMP = Energy Price + Congestion Price + Loss Price\n```\n\n### 1. Energy Price (λ_energy)\n- Marginal cost of generation\n- Same across all buses in uncongested network\n- Typically the cost of the marginal generator\n\n### 2. Congestion Price (λ_congestion)\n- Cost due to transmission constraints\n- Non-zero when lines are at thermal limits\n- Can be positive or negative\n\n### 3. Loss Price (λ_loss)\n- Cost due to transmission losses\n- Zero in DC-OPF (lossless approximation)\n- Non-zero in AC-OPF\n\n## LMP in DC-OPF\nIn DC-OPF, LMP = Energy Price + Congestion Price (no losses).\n\n### Mathematical Derivation\nConsider the power balance constraint at bus n:\n```\nΣ Pg[i] - Pd[n] = Σ Pf[l]\n i∈n              l∈n\n```\n\nThe Lagrangian includes:\n```\nL = Cost + Σ λ[n] * (balance constraint at bus n)\n```\n\nThe LMP at bus n is `λ[n]`, the dual variable of the power balance constraint.\n\n## Extracting LMPs from Optimization\n\n### Using CVXPY\n```python\nimport cvxpy as cp\n\n# Power balance constraints (keep references)\npower_balance_constraints = []\n\nfor n in range(n_buses):\n    # Generation at bus n\n    gen_at_bus = cp.sum([Pg[i] for i in range(n_gen) if gen_bus[i] == n])\n\n    # Demand at bus n\n    demand = bus_demand[n]\n\n    # Net injection\n    net_injection = gen_at_bus - demand\n\n    # Flow balance\n    flow_out = ... # DC power flow calculation\n\n    # Create and store constraint\n    constraint = (net_injection == flow_out)\n    power_balance_constraints.append(constraint)\n    constraints.append(constraint)\n\n# Solve problem\nproblem = cp.Problem(cp.Minimize(cost), constraints)\nproblem.solve()\n\n# Extract LMPs as dual variables\nlmps = [c.dual_value for c in power_balance_constraints]\n\n# LMPs are in $/MWh\nprint(f\"LMP at bus 0: ${lmps[0]:.2f}/MWh\")\n```\n\n### Important Notes\n1. **Sign Convention**: CVXPY dual variables follow the constraint formulation\n   - For `A*x == b`, dual value is the sensitivity to increasing b\n   - For `A*x >= b`, dual value is non-negative (from KKT conditions)\n\n2. **Units**: LMPs are in the same units as the objective function\n   - If cost is in $/hr and power in MW, LMP is in $/MWh\n\n3. **Constraint Formulation**: Be consistent with how you write power balance\n   ```python\n   # Option 1: generation - demand = injection\n   constraint = (gen - demand == injection)\n   lmp = constraint.dual_value  # Positive LMP\n\n   # Option 2: demand - generation = -injection\n   constraint = (demand - gen == -injection)\n   lmp = -constraint.dual_value  # Need to negate\n   ```\n\n## Interpreting LMPs\n\n### Uncongested Network\n- All buses have the same LMP\n- LMP equals the marginal cost of the most expensive operating generator\n- No congestion charges\n\n### Congested Network\n- LMPs vary by location\n- Higher LMPs downstream of congestion (import-constrained)\n- Lower LMPs upstream of congestion (export-constrained)\n\n### Example\n```\nGen 1 ($20/MWh) → Bus 1 → Line (100 MW limit) → Bus 2 → Load\nGen 2 ($50/MWh) → Bus 2\n```\n\nIf line is congested:\n- LMP at Bus 1: $20/MWh (marginal cost of Gen 1)\n- LMP at Bus 2: $50/MWh (marginal cost of Gen 2)\n- Congestion rent: (50-20) × 100 MW = $3000/hr\n\n## Congestion Revenue\nWhen lines are congested, the ISO collects congestion revenue:\n```\nCongestion Revenue = Σ (LMP[to] - LMP[from]) × Flow[line]\n                     for all congested lines\n```\n\nThis revenue is typically distributed to transmission rights holders.\n\n## Practical Implementation\n\n```python\ndef calculate_lmps_and_congestion(result, buses, branches):\n    \"\"\"\n    Extract LMPs and identify congested lines\n\n    Parameters:\n    - result: Optimization result with dual variables\n    - buses: Bus data\n    - branches: Branch data\n\n    Returns:\n    - lmps: Array of LMPs by bus\n    - congested_lines: List of congested line details\n    \"\"\"\n    n_buses = len(buses)\n\n    # Extract LMPs from dual variables\n    lmps = np.array([result['power_balance'][i].dual_value\n                     for i in range(n_buses)])\n\n    # Identify congested lines (at thermal limit)\n    congested_lines = []\n    for i, branch in enumerate(branches):\n        from_bus = int(branch[0]) - 1\n        to_bus = int(branch[1]) - 1\n        flow = result['branch_flows'][i]\n        limit = branch[5]  # RATE_A\n\n        # Check if binding (>= 99% of limit)\n        if limit > 0 and abs(flow) >= 0.99 * limit:\n            congested_lines.append({\n                'from': int(branch[0]),\n                'to': int(branch[1]),\n                'flow_MW': float(flow),\n                'limit_MW': float(limit),\n                'utilization': abs(flow) / limit,\n                'lmp_from': lmps[from_bus],\n                'lmp_to': lmps[to_bus],\n                'congestion_price': lmps[to_bus] - lmps[from_bus]\n            })\n\n    return lmps, congested_lines\n```\n\n## Sensitivity Analysis Using LMPs\n\n### Load Increase Impact\nIf load at bus n increases by ΔL:\n```\nΔCost ≈ LMP[n] × ΔL\n```\n\n### Generation Increase Impact\nIf generation at bus n increases by ΔG:\n```\nΔCost ≈ -LMP[n] × ΔG\n```\n\n### Line Capacity Increase Impact\nMore complex; requires resolving or using sensitivity analysis:\n```\n∂Cost/∂(Line Capacity) = μ[line]\n```\nwhere μ[line] is the dual variable of the line flow constraint.\n\n## Market Applications\n\n### Generator Revenue\n```\nRevenue[i] = LMP[bus(i)] × Pg[i]\nProfit[i] = Revenue[i] - Cost[i]\n```\n\n### Consumer Cost\n```\nCost[n] = LMP[n] × Load[n]\n```\n\n### Arbitrage Opportunities\nIf LMP[A] < LMP[B] and transmission is available, energy can be profitably moved from A to B.\n\n## Validation Checks\n\n```python\ndef validate_lmps(lmps, generators, gencosts):\n    \"\"\"Validate that LMPs make economic sense\"\"\"\n\n    # Check 1: LMPs should be non-negative\n    assert all(lmp >= -0.01 for lmp in lmps), \"Negative LMPs detected\"\n\n    # Check 2: Minimum LMP should be close to cheapest marginal cost\n    min_marginal_cost = min(gc[5] for gc in gencosts if gc[0] == 2)\n    min_lmp = min(lmps)\n    assert min_lmp >= min_marginal_cost * 0.9, \\\n        \"Min LMP below cheapest generator cost\"\n\n    # Check 3: Maximum LMP should not exceed most expensive operating gen\n    max_marginal_cost = max(gc[5] for gc in gencosts if gc[0] == 2)\n    max_lmp = max(lmps)\n    assert max_lmp <= max_marginal_cost * 1.5, \\\n        \"Max LMP suspiciously high\"\n\n    print(\"LMP validation passed\")\n    print(f\"LMP range: ${min_lmp:.2f} - ${max_lmp:.2f}/MWh\")\n```\n\n## References\n- Schweppe, F. They are fundamental to electricity market operations and congestion management.\n\n## Definition\nThe LMP at bus n is:\n```\nLMP[n] = ∂(Total Cost) / ∂(Load[n])\n```\n\nIn optimization terms, LMPs are the dual variables (shadow prices) of the power balance constraints.\n\n## Components of LMP\nLMP can be decomposed into three components:\n\n```\nLMP = Energy Price + Congestion Price + Loss Price\n```\n\n### 1. Energy Price (λ_energy)\n- Marginal cost of generation\n- Same across all buses in uncongested network\n- Typically the cost of the marginal generator\n\n### 2. Congestion Price (λ_congestion)\n- Cost due to transmission constraints\n- Non-zero when lines are at thermal limits\n- Can be positive or negative\n\n### 3. Loss Price (λ_loss)\n- Cost due to transmission losses\n- Zero in DC-OPF (lossless approximation)\n- Non-zero in AC-OPF\n\n## LMP in DC-OPF\nIn DC-OPF, LMP = Energy Price + Congestion Price (no losses).\n\n### Mathematical Derivation\nConsider the power balance constraint at bus n:\n```\nΣ Pg[i] - Pd[n] = Σ Pf[l]\n i∈n              l∈n\n```\n\nThe Lagrangian includes:\n```\nL = Cost + Σ λ[n] * (balance constraint at bus n)\n```\n\nThe LMP at bus n is `λ[n]`, the dual variable of the power balance constraint.\n\n## Extracting LMPs from Optimization\n\n### Using CVXPY\n```python\nimport cvxpy as cp\n\n# Power balance constraints (keep references)\npower_balance_constraints = []\n\nfor n in range(n_buses):\n    # Generation at bus n\n    gen_at_bus = cp.sum([Pg[i] for i in range(n_gen) if gen_bus[i] == n])\n\n    # Demand at bus n\n    demand = bus_demand[n]\n\n    # Net injection\n    net_injection = gen_at_bus - demand\n\n    # Flow balance\n    flow_out = ... # DC power flow calculation\n\n    # Create and store constraint\n    constraint = (net_injection == flow_out)\n    power_balance_constraints.append(constraint)\n    constraints.append(constraint)\n\n# Solve problem\nproblem = cp.Problem(cp.Minimize(cost), constraints)\nproblem.solve()\n\n# Extract LMPs as dual variables\nlmps = [c.dual_value for c in power_balance_constraints]\n\n# LMPs are in $/MWh\nprint(f\"LMP at bus 0: ${lmps[0]:.2f}/MWh\")\n```\n\n### Important Notes\n1. **Sign Convention**: CVXPY dual variables follow the constraint formulation\n   - For `A*x == b`, dual value is the sensitivity to increasing b\n   - For `A*x >= b`, dual value is non-negative (from KKT conditions)\n\n2. **Units**: LMPs are in the same units as the objective function\n   - If cost is in $/hr and power in MW, LMP is in $/MWh\n\n3. **Constraint Formulation**: Be consistent with how you write power balance\n   ```python\n   # Option 1: generation - demand = injection\n   constraint = (gen - demand == injection)\n   lmp = constraint.dual_value  # Positive LMP\n\n   # Option 2: demand - generation = -injection\n   constraint = (demand - gen == -injection)\n   lmp = -constraint.dual_value  # Need to negate\n   ```\n\n## Interpreting LMPs\n\n### Uncongested Network\n- All buses have the same LMP\n- LMP equals the marginal cost of the most expensive operating generator\n- No congestion charges\n\n### Congested Network\n- LMPs vary by location\n- Higher LMPs downstream of congestion (import-constrained)\n- Lower LMPs upstream of congestion (export-constrained)\n\n### Example\n```\nGen 1 ($20/MWh) → Bus 1 → Line (100 MW limit) → Bus 2 → Load\nGen 2 ($50/MWh) → Bus 2\n```\n\nIf line is congested:\n- LMP at Bus 1: $20/MWh (marginal cost of Gen 1)\n- LMP at Bus 2: $50/MWh (marginal cost of Gen 2)\n- Congestion rent: (50-20) × 100 MW = $3000/hr\n\n## Congestion Revenue\nWhen lines are congested, the ISO collects congestion revenue:\n```\nCongestion Revenue = Σ (LMP[to] - LMP[from]) × Flow[line]\n                     for all congested lines\n```\n\nThis revenue is typically distributed to transmission rights holders.\n\n## Practical Implementation\n\n```python\ndef calculate_lmps_and_congestion(result, buses, branches):\n    \"\"\"\n    Extract LMPs and identify congested lines\n\n    Parameters:\n    - result: Optimization result with dual variables\n    - buses: Bus data\n    - branches: Branch data\n\n    Returns:\n    - lmps: Array of LMPs by bus\n    - congested_lines: List of congested line details\n    \"\"\"\n    n_buses = len(buses)\n\n    # Extract LMPs from dual variables\n    lmps = np.array([result['power_balance'][i].dual_value\n                     for i in range(n_buses)])\n\n    # Identify congested lines (at thermal limit)\n    congested_lines = []\n    for i, branch in enumerate(branches):\n        from_bus = int(branch[0]) - 1\n        to_bus = int(branch[1]) - 1\n        flow = result['branch_flows'][i]\n        limit = branch[5]  # RATE_A\n\n        # Check if binding (>= 99% of limit)\n        if limit > 0 and abs(flow) >= 0.99 * limit:\n            congested_lines.append({\n                'from': int(branch[0]),\n                'to': int(branch[1]),\n                'flow_MW': float(flow),\n                'limit_MW': float(limit),\n                'utilization': abs(flow) / limit,\n                'lmp_from': lmps[from_bus],\n                'lmp_to': lmps[to_bus],\n                'congestion_price': lmps[to_bus] - lmps[from_bus]\n            })\n\n    return lmps, congested_lines\n```\n\n## Sensitivity Analysis Using LMPs\n\n### Load Increase Impact\nIf load at bus n increases by ΔL:\n```\nΔCost ≈ LMP[n] × ΔL\n```\n\n### Generation Increase Impact\nIf generation at bus n increases by ΔG:\n```\nΔCost ≈ -LMP[n] × ΔG\n```\n\n### Line Capacity Increase Impact\nMore complex; requires resolving or using sensitivity analysis:\n```\n∂Cost/∂(Line Capacity) = μ[line]\n```\nwhere μ[line] is the dual variable of the line flow constraint.\n\n## Market Applications\n\n### Generator Revenue\n```\nRevenue[i] = LMP[bus(i)] × Pg[i]\nProfit[i] = Revenue[i] - Cost[i]\n```\n\n### Consumer Cost\n```\nCost[n] = LMP[n] × Load[n]\n```\n\n### Arbitrage Opportunities\nIf LMP[A] < LMP[B] and transmission is available, energy can be profitably moved from A to B.\n\n## Validation Checks\n\n```python\ndef validate_lmps(lmps, generators, gencosts):\n    \"\"\"Validate that LMPs make economic sense\"\"\"\n\n    # Check 1: LMPs should be non-negative\n    assert all(lmp >= -0.01 for lmp in lmps), \"Negative LMPs detected\"\n\n    # Check 2: Minimum LMP should be close to cheapest marginal cost\n    min_marginal_cost = min(gc[5] for gc in gencosts if gc[0] == 2)\n    min_lmp = min(lmps)\n    assert min_lmp >= min_marginal_cost * 0.9, \\\n        \"Min LMP below cheapest generator cost\"\n\n    # Check 3: Maximum LMP should not exceed most expensive operating gen\n    max_marginal_cost = max(gc[5] for gc in gencosts if gc[0] == 2)\n    max_lmp = max(lmps)\n    assert max_lmp <= max_marginal_cost * 1.5, \\\n        \"Max LMP suspiciously high\"\n\n    print(\"LMP validation passed\")\n    print(f\"LMP range: ${min_lmp:.2f} - ${max_lmp:.2f}/MWh\")\n```\n\n## References\n- Schweppe, F. buses: Bus data matrix (MATPOWER format)\n    - generators: Generator data matrix\n    - branches: Branch data matrix\n    - gencosts: Generator cost data\n    - reserve_capacity: Maximum reserve capacity per generator (MW)\n    - reserve_requirement: Minimum system reserve requirement (MW)\n    - baseMVA: Base MVA for per-unit system\n    - line_capacity_adjustments: Dict of {(from_bus, to_bus): capacity_multiplier}\n\n    Returns:\n    - Dictionary with optimization results\n    \"\"\"\n    n_bus = len(buses)\n    n_gen = len(generators)\n    n_branch = len(branches)\n\n    print(f\"Solving DC-OPF with reserves:\")\n    print(f\"  Buses: {n_bus}, Generators: {n_gen}, Branches: {n_branch}\")\n    print(f\"  Reserve requirement: {reserve_requirement:.1f} MW\")\n\n    # Decision variables\n    Pg = cp.Variable(n_gen, name=\"Pg\")  # Generator power output\n    R = cp.Variable(n_gen, name=\"R\")    # Reserve provision\n    theta = cp.Variable(n_bus, name=\"theta\")  # Voltage angles\n\n    # Build cost function\n    energy_cost_terms = []\n    reserve_cost_terms = []\n\n    for i in range(n_gen):\n        # Energy cost (quadratic: c2*P^2 + c1*P + c0)\n        if gencosts[i][0] == 2:  # Polynomial model\n            ncost = int(gencosts[i][3])\n            if ncost == 3:\n                c2 = gencosts[i][4]\n                c1 = gencosts[i][5]\n                c0 = gencosts[i][6]\n                energy_cost_terms.append(c2 * cp.power(Pg[i], 2) + c1 * Pg[i] + c0)\n            elif ncost == 2:\n                c1 = gencosts[i][4]\n                c0 = gencosts[i][5]\n                energy_cost_terms.append(c1 * Pg[i] + c0)\n            else:\n                print(f\"Warning: Unexpected ncost={ncost} for generator {i}\")\n\n        # Reserve cost (typically 10% of marginal energy cost)\n        marginal_cost = gencosts[i][5] if gencosts[i][0] == 2 else 0\n        reserve_price = marginal_cost * 0.1  # 10% of energy marginal cost\n        reserve_cost_terms.append(reserve_price * R[i])\n\n    total_cost = cp.sum(energy_cost_terms) + cp.sum(reserve_cost_terms)\n\n    # Constraints\n    constraints = []\n\n    # 1. Generator limits\n    for i in range(n_gen):\n        if generators[i][7] == 1:  # Generator is in service\n            Pmin = generators[i][9]\n            Pmax = generators[i][8]\n            constraints.append(Pg[i] >= Pmin)\n            constraints.append(Pg[i] <= Pmax)\n        else:\n            constraints.append(Pg[i] == 0)\n\n    # 2. Reserve constraints\n    for i in range(n_gen):\n        # Non-negativity\n        constraints.append(R[i] >= 0)\n\n        # Reserve capacity limit\n        if i < len(reserve_capacity):\n            constraints.append(R[i] <= reserve_capacity[i])\n        else:\n            constraints.append(R[i] <= 0)\n\n        # Energy-reserve coupling (headroom constraint)\n        if generators[i][7] == 1:\n            Pmax = generators[i][8]\n            constraints.append(Pg[i] + R[i] <= Pmax)\n\n    # 3. System reserve requirement\n    reserve_constraint = cp.sum(R) >= reserve_requirement\n    constraints.append(reserve_constraint)\n\n    # 4. Build branch flow matrices\n    # For DC power flow: Pf = B * (theta_from - theta_to)\n    from_bus = np.zeros(n_branch, dtype=int)\n    to_bus = np.zeros(n_branch, dtype=int)\n    B_branch = np.zeros(n_branch)  # Branch susceptance\n    thermal_limits = np.zeros(n_branch)\n\n    for i in range(n_branch):\n        from_bus[i] = int(branches[i][0]) - 1  # Convert to 0-indexed\n        to_bus[i] = int(branches[i][1]) - 1\n        X = branches[i][3]  # Reactance\n        if X != 0:\n            B_branch[i] = 1.0 / X\n        else:\n            B_branch[i] = 0.0\n\n        # Thermal limit (RATE_A)\n        thermal_limits[i] = branches[i][5]\n\n        # Apply capacity adjustment if specified\n        if line_capacity_adjustments:\n            from_bus_orig = int(branches[i][0])\n            to_bus_orig = int(branches[i][1])\n            if (from_bus_orig, to_bus_orig) in line_capacity_adjustments:\n                multiplier = line_capacity_adjustments[(from_bus_orig, to_bus_orig)]\n                thermal_limits[i] *= multiplier\n                print(f\"  Adjusting line {from_bus_orig}->{to_bus_orig}: \" +\n                      f\"{branches[i][5]:.1f} -> {thermal_limits[i]:.1f} MW\")\n            elif (to_bus_orig, from_bus_orig) in line_capacity_adjustments:\n                multiplier = line_capacity_adjustments[(to_bus_orig, from_bus_orig)]\n                thermal_limits[i] *= multiplier\n                print(f\"  Adjusting line {to_bus_orig}->{from_bus_orig}: \" +\n                      f\"{branches[i][5]:.1f} -> {thermal_limits[i]:.1f} MW\")\n\n    # 5. Power balance constraints at each bus\n    power_balance_constraints = []\n\n    for n in range(n_bus):\n        # Generation at this bus\n        gen_at_bus = 0\n        for i in range(n_gen):\n            if int(generators[i][0]) - 1 == n:\n                gen_at_bus += Pg[i]\n\n        # Demand at this bus\n        demand = buses[n][2]\n\n        # Net injection = generation - demand\n        net_injection = gen_at_bus - demand\n\n        # Flow out of bus (using DC power flow)\n        flow_out = 0\n        for l in range(n_branch):\n            if branches[l][10] == 1:  # Branch is in service\n                if from_bus[l] == n:\n                    # Flow out on lines starting from this bus\n                    flow_out += B_branch[l] * (theta[n] - theta[to_bus[l]])\n                elif to_bus[l] == n:\n                    # Flow in on lines ending at this bus (negative flow out)\n                    flow_out -= B_branch[l] * (theta[from_bus[l]] - theta[n])\n\n        # Power balance: net injection = flow out\n        constraint = (net_injection == flow_out)\n        power_balance_constraints.append(constraint)\n        constraints.append(constraint)\n\n    # 6. Transmission line thermal limits\n    line_flow_constraints = []\n    for l in range(n_branch):\n        if branches[l][10] == 1:  # Branch is in service\n            flow = B_branch[l] * (theta[from_bus[l]] - theta[to_bus[l]])\n\n            if thermal_limits[l] > 0:\n                # Bidirectional limit\n                constraints.append(flow <= thermal_limits[l])\n                constraints.append(flow >= -thermal_limits[l])\n                line_flow_constraints.append(flow)\n            else:\n                line_flow_constraints.append(flow)\n        else:\n            line_flow_constraints.append(0)\n\n    # 7. Reference angle (slack bus)\n    slack_bus = 0\n    for i in range(n_bus):\n        if buses[i][1] == 3:  # Slack bus type\n            slack_bus = i\n            break\n    constraints.append(theta[slack_bus] == 0)\n\n    # Solve the optimization problem\n    problem = cp.Problem(cp.Minimize(total_cost), constraints)\n\n    print(\"  Solving optimization problem...\")\n    problem.solve(solver=cp.ECOS, max_iters=200, verbose=False)\n\n    if problem.status not in [cp.OPTIMAL, cp.OPTIMAL_INACCURATE]:\n        print(f\"  Warning: Solver status = {problem.status}\")\n\n    # buses: Bus data matrix (MATPOWER format)\n    - generators: Generator data matrix\n    - branches: Branch data matrix\n    - gencosts: Generator cost data\n    - reserve_capacity: Maximum reserve capacity per generator (MW)\n    - reserve_requirement: Minimum system reserve requirement (MW)\n    - baseMVA: Base MVA for per-unit system\n    - line_capacity_adjustments: Dict of {(from_bus, to_bus): capacity_multiplier}\n\n    Returns:\n    - Dictionary with optimization results\n    \"\"\"\n    n_bus = len(buses)\n    n_gen = len(generators)\n    n_branch = len(branches)\n\n    print(f\"Solving DC-OPF with reserves:\")\n    print(f\"  Buses: {n_bus}, Generators: {n_gen}, Branches: {n_branch}\")\n    print(f\"  Reserve requirement: {reserve_requirement:.1f} MW\")\n\n    # Decision variables\n    Pg = cp.Variable(n_gen, name=\"Pg\")  # Generator power output\n    R = cp.Variable(n_gen, name=\"R\")    # Reserve provision\n    theta = cp.Variable(n_bus, name=\"theta\")  # Voltage angles\n\n    # Build cost function\n    energy_cost_terms = []\n    reserve_cost_terms = []\n\n    for i in range(n_gen):\n        # Energy cost (quadratic: c2*P^2 + c1*P + c0)\n        if gencosts[i][0] == 2:  # Polynomial model\n            ncost = int(gencosts[i][3])\n            if ncost == 3:\n                c2 = gencosts[i][4]\n                c1 = gencosts[i][5]\n                c0 = gencosts[i][6]\n                energy_cost_terms.append(c2 * cp.power(Pg[i], 2) + c1 * Pg[i] + c0)\n            elif ncost == 2:\n                c1 = gencosts[i][4]\n                c0 = gencosts[i][5]\n                energy_cost_terms.append(c1 * Pg[i] + c0)\n            else:\n                print(f\"Warning: Unexpected ncost={ncost} for generator {i}\")\n\n        # Reserve cost (typically 10% of marginal energy cost)\n        marginal_cost = gencosts[i][5] if gencosts[i][0] == 2 else 0\n        reserve_price = marginal_cost * 0.1  # 10% of energy marginal cost\n        reserve_cost_terms.append(reserve_price * R[i])\n\n    total_cost = cp.sum(energy_cost_terms) + cp.sum(reserve_cost_terms)\n\n    # Constraints\n    constraints = []\n\n    # 1. Generator limits\n    for i in range(n_gen):\n        if generators[i][7] == 1:  # Generator is in service\n            Pmin = generators[i][9]\n            Pmax = generators[i][8]\n            constraints.append(Pg[i] >= Pmin)\n            constraints.append(Pg[i] <= Pmax)\n        else:\n            constraints.append(Pg[i] == 0)\n\n    # 2. Reserve constraints\n    for i in range(n_gen):\n        # Non-negativity\n        constraints.append(R[i] >= 0)\n\n        # Reserve capacity limit\n        if i < len(reserve_capacity):\n            constraints.append(R[i] <= reserve_capacity[i])\n        else:\n            constraints.append(R[i] <= 0)\n\n        # Energy-reserve coupling (headroom constraint)\n        if generators[i][7] == 1:\n            Pmax = generators[i][8]\n            constraints.append(Pg[i] + R[i] <= Pmax)\n\n    # 3. System reserve requirement\n    reserve_constraint = cp.sum(R) >= reserve_requirement\n    constraints.append(reserve_constraint)\n\n    # 4. Build branch flow matrices\n    # For DC power flow: Pf = B * (theta_from - theta_to)\n    from_bus = np.zeros(n_branch, dtype=int)\n    to_bus = np.zeros(n_branch, dtype=int)\n    B_branch = np.zeros(n_branch)  # Branch susceptance\n    thermal_limits = np.zeros(n_branch)\n\n    for i in range(n_branch):\n        from_bus[i] = int(branches[i][0]) - 1  # Convert to 0-indexed\n        to_bus[i] = int(branches[i][1]) - 1\n        X = branches[i][3]  # Reactance\n        if X != 0:\n            B_branch[i] = 1.0 / X\n        else:\n            B_branch[i] = 0.0\n\n        # Thermal limit (RATE_A)\n        thermal_limits[i] = branches[i][5]\n\n        # Apply capacity adjustment if specified\n        if line_capacity_adjustments:\n            from_bus_orig = int(branches[i][0])\n            to_bus_orig = int(branches[i][1])\n            if (from_bus_orig, to_bus_orig) in line_capacity_adjustments:\n                multiplier = line_capacity_adjustments[(from_bus_orig, to_bus_orig)]\n                thermal_limits[i] *= multiplier\n                print(f\"  Adjusting line {from_bus_orig}->{to_bus_orig}: \" +\n                      f\"{branches[i][5]:.1f} -> {thermal_limits[i]:.1f} MW\")\n            elif (to_bus_orig, from_bus_orig) in line_capacity_adjustments:\n                multiplier = line_capacity_adjustments[(to_bus_orig, from_bus_orig)]\n                thermal_limits[i] *= multiplier\n                print(f\"  Adjusting line {to_bus_orig}->{from_bus_orig}: \" +\n                      f\"{branches[i][5]:.1f} -> {thermal_limits[i]:.1f} MW\")\n\n    # 5. Power balance constraints at each bus\n    power_balance_constraints = []\n\n    for n in range(n_bus):\n        # Generation at this bus\n        gen_at_bus = 0\n        for i in range(n_gen):\n            if int(generators[i][0]) - 1 == n:\n                gen_at_bus += Pg[i]\n\n        # Demand at this bus\n        demand = buses[n][2]\n\n        # Net injection = generation - demand\n        net_injection = gen_at_bus - demand\n\n        # Flow out of bus (using DC power flow)\n        flow_out = 0\n        for l in range(n_branch):\n            if branches[l][10] == 1:  # Branch is in service\n                if from_bus[l] == n:\n                    # Flow out on lines starting from this bus\n                    flow_out += B_branch[l] * (theta[n] - theta[to_bus[l]])\n                elif to_bus[l] == n:\n                    # Flow in on lines ending at this bus (negative flow out)\n                    flow_out -= B_branch[l] * (theta[from_bus[l]] - theta[n])\n\n        # Power balance: net injection = flow out\n        constraint = (net_injection == flow_out)\n        power_balance_constraints.append(constraint)\n        constraints.append(constraint)\n\n    # 6. Transmission line thermal limits\n    line_flow_constraints = []\n    for l in range(n_branch):\n        if branches[l][10] == 1:  # Branch is in service\n            flow = B_branch[l] * (theta[from_bus[l]] - theta[to_bus[l]])\n\n            if thermal_limits[l] > 0:\n                # Bidirectional limit\n                constraints.append(flow <= thermal_limits[l])\n                constraints.append(flow >= -thermal_limits[l])\n                line_flow_constraints.append(flow)\n            else:\n                line_flow_constraints.append(flow)\n        else:\n            line_flow_constraints.append(0)\n\n    # 7. Reference angle (slack bus)\n    slack_bus = 0\n    for i in range(n_bus):\n        if buses[i][1] == 3:  # Slack bus type\n            slack_bus = i\n            break\n    constraints.append(theta[slack_bus] == 0)\n\n    # Solve the optimization problem\n    problem = cp.Problem(cp.Minimize(total_cost), constraints)\n\n    print(\"  Solving optimization problem...\")\n    problem.solve(solver=cp.ECOS, max_iters=200, verbose=False)\n\n    if problem.status not in [cp.OPTIMAL, cp.OPTIMAL_INACCURATE]:\n        print(f\"  Warning: Solver status = {problem.status}\")\n\n    # Extract results\n    lmps = np.array([c.dual_value if c.dual_value is not None else 0.0\n                     for c in power_balance_constraints])\n\n    reserve_mcp = reserve_constraint.dual_value if reserve_constraint.dual_value else 0.0\n\n    # Calculate actual line flows\n    branch_flows = []\n    for l in range(n_branch):\n        if branches[l][10] == 1:\n            flow = B_branch[l] * (theta.value[from_bus[l]] - theta.value[to_bus[l]])\n            branch_flows.append(flow)\n        else:\n            branch_flows.append(0.0)\n\n    # Identify binding lines (>= 99% of capacity)\n    binding_lines = []\n    for l in range(n_branch):\n        if thermal_limits[l] > 0:\n            utilization = abs(branch_flows[l]) / thermal_limits[l]\n            if utilization >= 0.99:\n                binding_lines.append({\n                    'from': int(branches[l][0]),\n                    'to': int(branches[l][1]),\n                    'flow_MW': float(branch_flows[l]),\n                    'limit_MW': float(thermal_limits[l]),\n                    'utilization': float(utilization)\n                })\n\n    print(f\"  Status: {problem.status}\")\n    print(f\"  Total cost: ${problem.value:,.2f}/hr\")\n    print(f\"  Reserve MCP: ${reserve_mcp:.2f}/MWh\")\n    print(f\"  Binding lines: {len(binding_lines)}\")\n\n    return {\n        'status': problem.status,\n        'total_cost': problem.value,\n        'Pg': Pg.value,\n        'R': R.value,\n        'theta': theta.value,\n        'lmps': lmps,\n        'reserve_mcp': reserve_mcp,\n        'branch_flows': branch_flows,\n        'binding_lines': binding_lines,\n        'power_balance_constraints': power_balance_constraints\n    }\n\n\ndef main():\n    \"\"\"Main analysis workflow\"\"\"\n    print(\"=\" * 70)\n    print(\"Market Clearing Analysis: Base vs. Counterfactual Scenario\")\n    print(\"=\" * 70)\n\n    # Save report\n    with open('report.json', 'w') as f:\n        json.dump(report, f, indent=2)\n\n    print(\"Report saved to report.json\")\n\n    # Print summary\n    print(\"\\n\" + \"=\" * 70)\n    print(\"SUMMARY\")\n    print(\"=\" * 70)\n    print(f\"Base case total cost: ${base_result['total_cost']:,.2f}/hr\")\n    print(f\"Counterfactual total cost: ${cf_result['total_cost']:,.2f}/hr\")\n    print(f\"Cost savings: ${cost_reduction:,.2f}/hr\")\n    print(f\"LMP range (base): ${min(base_result['lmps']):.2f} - ${max(base_result['lmps']):.2f}/MWh\")\n    print(f\"LMP range (counterfactual): ${min(cf_result['lmps']):.2f} - ${max(cf_result['lmps']):.2f}/MWh\")\n    print(f\"Binding lines (base): {len(base_result['binding_lines'])}\")\n    print(f\"Binding lines (counterfactual): {len(cf_result['binding_lines'])}\")\n    print(\"=\" * 70)\n\n\nif __name__ == \"__main__\":\n    main()\n","structuredPatch":[],"originalFile":null}}

problem.solve(solver=cp.OSQP, verbose=False, eps_abs=1e-4, eps_rel=1e-4)","originalFile":"#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"\nDC-OPF with Reserve Co-optimization for Market Clearing Analysis\n\nThis script performs market clearing optimization for a power system,\ncomparing a base case with a counterfactual scenario where a specific\ntransmission line capacity is increased by 20%.\n\"\"\"\n\nimport json\nimport numpy as np\nimport cvxpy as cp\nfrom typing import Dict, List, Tuple\n\n\ndef load_network_data(filename: str) -> Dict:\n    \"\"\"Load MATPOWER network data from JSON file\"\"\"\n    with open(filename, 'r') as f:\n        data = json.load(f)\n    return data\n\n\ndef solve_dcopf_with_reserves(\n    buses: np.ndarray,\n    generators: np.ndarray,\n    branches: np.ndarray,\n    gencosts: np.ndarray,\n    reserve_capacity: List[float],\n    reserve_requirement: float,\n    baseMVA: float = 100.0,\n    line_capacity_adjustments: Dict[Tuple[int, int], float] = None\n) -> Dict:\n    \"\"\"\n    Solve DC-OPF with reserve co-optimization\n\n    Parameters:\n    - buses: Bus data matrix (MATPOWER format)\n    - generators: Generator data matrix\n    - branches: Branch data matrix\n    - gencosts: Generator cost data\n    - reserve_capacity: Maximum reserve capacity per generator (MW)\n    - reserve_requirement: Minimum system reserve requirement (MW)\n    - baseMVA: Base MVA for per-unit system\n    - line_capacity_adjustments: Dict of {(from_bus, to_bus): capacity_multiplier}\n\n    Returns:\n    - Dictionary with optimization results\n    \"\"\"\n    n_bus = len(buses)\n    n_gen = len(generators)\n    n_branch = len(branches)\n\n    print(f\"Solving DC-OPF with reserves:\")\n    print(f\"  Buses: {n_bus}, Generators: {n_gen}, Branches: {n_branch}\")\n    print(f\"  Reserve requirement: {reserve_requirement:.1f} MW\")\n\n    # Decision variables\n    Pg = cp.Variable(n_gen, name=\"Pg\")  # Generator power output\n    R = cp.Variable(n_gen, name=\"R\")    # Reserve provision\n    theta = cp.Variable(n_bus, name=\"theta\")  # Voltage angles\n\n    # Build cost function\n    energy_cost_terms = []\n    reserve_cost_terms = []\n\n    for i in range(n_gen):\n        # Energy cost (quadratic: c2*P^2 + c1*P + c0)\n        if gencosts[i][0] == 2:  # Polynomial model\n            ncost = int(gencosts[i][3])\n            if ncost == 3:\n                c2 = gencosts[i][4]\n                c1 = gencosts[i][5]\n                c0 = gencosts[i][6]\n                energy_cost_terms.append(c2 * cp.power(Pg[i], 2) + c1 * Pg[i] + c0)\n            elif ncost == 2:\n                c1 = gencosts[i][4]\n                c0 = gencosts[i][5]\n                energy_cost_terms.append(c1 * Pg[i] + c0)\n            else:\n                print(f\"Warning: Unexpected ncost={ncost} for generator {i}\")\n\n        # Reserve cost (typically 10% of marginal energy cost)\n        marginal_cost = gencosts[i][5] if gencosts[i][0] == 2 else 0\n        reserve_price = marginal_cost * 0.1  # 10% of energy marginal cost\n        reserve_cost_terms.append(reserve_price * R[i])\n\n    total_cost = cp.sum(energy_cost_terms) + cp.sum(reserve_cost_terms)\n\n    # Constraints\n    constraints = []\n\n    # 1. Generator limits\n    for i in range(n_gen):\n        if generators[i][7] == 1:  # Generator is in service\n            Pmin = generators[i][9]\n            Pmax = generators[i][8]\n            constraints.append(Pg[i] >= Pmin)\n            constraints.append(Pg[i] <= Pmax)\n        else:\n            constraints.append(Pg[i] == 0)\n\n    # 2. Reserve constraints\n    for i in range(n_gen):\n        # Non-negativity\n        constraints.append(R[i] >= 0)\n\n        # Reserve capacity limit\n        if i < len(reserve_capacity):\n            constraints.append(R[i] <= reserve_capacity[i])\n        else:\n            constraints.append(R[i] <= 0)\n\n        # Energy-reserve coupling (headroom constraint)\n        if generators[i][7] == 1:\n            Pmax = generators[i][8]\n            constraints.append(Pg[i] + R[i] <= Pmax)\n\n    # 3. System reserve requirement\n    reserve_constraint = cp.sum(R) >= reserve_requirement\n    constraints.append(reserve_constraint)\n\n    # 4. Build branch flow matrices\n    # For DC power flow: Pf = B * (theta_from - theta_to)\n    from_bus = np.zeros(n_branch, dtype=int)\n    to_bus = np.zeros(n_branch, dtype=int)\n    B_branch = np.zeros(n_branch)  # Branch susceptance\n    thermal_limits = np.zeros(n_branch)\n\n    for i in range(n_branch):\n        from_bus[i] = int(branches[i][0]) - 1  # Convert to 0-indexed\n        to_bus[i] = int(branches[i][1]) - 1\n        X = branches[i][3]  # Reactance\n        if X != 0:\n            B_branch[i] = 1.0 / X\n        else:\n            B_branch[i] = 0.0\n\n        # Thermal limit (RATE_A)\n        thermal_limits[i] = branches[i][5]\n\n        # Apply capacity adjustment if specified\n        if line_capacity_adjustments:\n            from_bus_orig = int(branches[i][0])\n            to_bus_orig = int(branches[i][1])\n            if (from_bus_orig, to_bus_orig) in line_capacity_adjustments:\n                multiplier = line_capacity_adjustments[(from_bus_orig, to_bus_orig)]\n                thermal_limits[i] *= multiplier\n                print(f\"  Adjusting line {from_bus_orig}->{to_bus_orig}: \" +\n                      f\"{branches[i][5]:.1f} -> {thermal_limits[i]:.1f} MW\")\n            elif (to_bus_orig, from_bus_orig) in line_capacity_adjustments:\n                multiplier = line_capacity_adjustments[(to_bus_orig, from_bus_orig)]\n                thermal_limits[i] *= multiplier\n                print(f\"  Adjusting line {to_bus_orig}->{from_bus_orig}: \" +\n                      f\"{branches[i][5]:.1f} -> {thermal_limits[i]:.1f} MW\")\n\n    # 5. Power balance constraints at each bus\n    power_balance_constraints = []\n\n    for n in range(n_bus):\n        # Generation at this bus\n        gen_at_bus = 0\n        for i in range(n_gen):\n            if int(generators[i][0]) - 1 == n:\n                gen_at_bus += Pg[i]\n\n        # Demand at this bus\n        demand = buses[n][2]\n\n        # Net injection = generation - demand\n        net_injection = gen_at_bus - demand\n\n        # Flow out of bus (using DC power flow)\n        flow_out = 0\n        for l in range(n_branch):\n            if branches[l][10] == 1:  # Branch is in service\n                if from_bus[l] == n:\n                    # Flow out on lines starting from this bus\n                    flow_out += B_branch[l] * (theta[n] - theta[to_bus[l]])\n                elif to_bus[l] == n:\n                    # Flow in on lines ending at this bus (negative flow out)\n                    flow_out -= B_branch[l] * (theta[from_bus[l]] - theta[n])\n\n        # Power balance: net injection = flow out\n        constraint = (net_injection == flow_out)\n        power_balance_constraints.append(constraint)\n        constraints.append(constraint)\n\n    # 6. Transmission line thermal limits\n    line_flow_constraints = []\n    for l in range(n_branch):\n        if branches[l][10] == 1:  # Branch is in service\n            flow = B_branch[l] * (theta[from_bus[l]] - theta[to_bus[l]])\n\n            if thermal_limits[l] > 0:\n                # Bidirectional limit\n                constraints.append(flow <= thermal_limits[l])\n                constraints.append(flow >= -thermal_limits[l])\n                line_flow_constraints.append(flow)\n            else:\n                line_flow_constraints.append(flow)\n        else:\n            line_flow_constraints.append(0)\n\n    # 7. Reference angle (slack bus)\n    slack_bus = 0\n    for i in range(n_bus):\n        if buses[i][1] == 3:  # Slack bus type\n            slack_bus = i\n            break\n    constraints.append(theta[slack_bus] == 0)\n\n    # Solve the optimization problem\n    problem = cp.Problem(cp.Minimize(total_cost), constraints)\n\n    print(\"  Solving optimization problem...\")\n    problem.solve(solver=cp.ECOS, max_iters=200, verbose=False)\n\n    if problem.status not in [cp.OPTIMAL, cp.OPTIMAL_INACCURATE]:\n        print(f\"  Warning: Solver status = {problem.status}\")\n\n    # Extract results\n    lmps = np.array([c.dual_value if c.dual_value is not None else 0.0\n                     for c in power_balance_constraints])\n\n    reserve_mcp = reserve_constraint.dual_value if reserve_constraint.dual_value else 0.0\n\n    # Calculate actual line flows\n    branch_flows = []\n    for l in range(n_branch):\n        if branches[l][10] == 1:\n            flow = B_branch[l] * (theta.value[from_bus[l]] - theta.value[to_bus[l]])\n            branch_flows.append(flow)\n        else:\n            branch_flows.append(0.0)\n\n    # Identify binding lines (>= 99% of capacity)\n    binding_lines = []\n    for l in range(n_branch):\n        if thermal_limits[l] > 0:\n            utilization = abs(branch_flows[l]) / thermal_limits[l]\n            if utilization >= 0.99:\n                binding_lines.append({\n                    'from': int(branches[l][0]),\n                    'to': int(branches[l][1]),\n                    'flow_MW': float(branch_flows[l]),\n                    'limit_MW': float(thermal_limits[l]),\n                    'utilization': float(utilization)\n                })\n\n    print(f\"  Status: {problem.status}\")\n    print(f\"  Total cost: ${problem.value:,.2f}/hr\")\n    print(f\"  Reserve MCP: ${reserve_mcp:.2f}/MWh\")\n    print(f\"  Binding lines: {len(binding_lines)}\")\n\n    return {\n        'status': problem.status,\n        'total_cost': problem.value,\n        'Pg': Pg.value,\n        'R': R.value,\n        'theta': theta.value,\n        'lmps': lmps,\n        'reserve_mcp': reserve_mcp,\n        'branch_flows': branch_flows,\n        'binding_lines': binding_lines,\n        'power_balance_constraints': power_balance_constraints\n    }\n\n\ndef main():\n    \"\"\"Main analysis workflow\"\"\"\n    print(\"=\" * 70)\n    print(\"Market Clearing Analysis: Base vs. Counterfactual Scenario\")\n    print(\"=\" * 70)\n\n    # Load network data\n    print(\"\\nLoading network data...\")\n    data = load_network_data('network.json')\n\n    buses = np.array(data['bus'])\n    generators = np.array(data['gen'])\n    branches = np.array(data['branch'])\n    gencosts = np.array(data['gencost'])\n    reserve_capacity = data['reserve_capacity']\n    reserve_requirement = data['reserve_requirement']\n    baseMVA = data['baseMVA']\n\n    print(f\"Network loaded:\")\n    print(f\"  Buses: {len(buses)}\")\n    print(f\"  Generators: {len(generators)}\")\n    print(f\"  Branches: {len(branches)}\")\n    print(f\"  Total demand: {sum(bus[2] for bus in buses):.1f} MW\")\n    print(f\"  Reserve requirement: {reserve_requirement:.1f} MW\")\n\n    # Find the target line (64 -> 1501)\n    target_line_idx = None\n    for i, branch in enumerate(branches):\n        if (int(branch[0]) == 64 and int(branch[1]) == 1501) or \\\n           (int(branch[0]) == 1501 and int(branch[1]) == 64):\n            target_line_idx = i\n            break\n\n    if target_line_idx is None:\n        print(\"\\nError: Could not find line between bus 64 and 1501\")\n        return\n\n    target_from = int(branches[target_line_idx][0])\n    target_to = int(branches[target_line_idx][1])\n    base_capacity = branches[target_line_idx][5]\n\n    print(f\"\\nTarget line: {target_from} -> {target_to}\")\n    print(f\"  Base capacity: {base_capacity:.1f} MW\")\n    print(f\"  Counterfactual capacity: {base_capacity * 1.2:.1f} MW (+20%)\")\n\n    # Solve base case\n    print(\"\\n\" + \"=\" * 70)\n    print(\"SOLVING BASE CASE\")\n    print(\"=\" * 70)\n    base_result = solve_dcopf_with_reserves(\n        buses, generators, branches, gencosts,\n        reserve_capacity, reserve_requirement, baseMVA\n    )\n\n    # Solve counterfactual case\n    print(\"\\n\" + \"=\" * 70)\n    print(\"SOLVING COUNTERFACTUAL CASE\")\n    print(\"=\" * 70)\n    cf_result = solve_dcopf_with_reserves(\n        buses, generators, branches, gencosts,\n        reserve_capacity, reserve_requirement, baseMVA,\n        line_capacity_adjustments={(target_from, target_to): 1.2}\n    )\n\n    # Perform impact analysis\n    print(\"\\n\" + \"=\" * 70)\n    print(\"IMPACT ANALYSIS\")\n    print(\"=\" * 70)\n\n    cost_reduction = base_result['total_cost'] - cf_result['total_cost']\n    print(f\"\\nCost reduction: ${cost_reduction:,.2f}/hr ({cost_reduction/base_result['total_cost']*100:.2f}%)\")\n\n    #
